People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Even stuff other people would probably just handle.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your apprenticeship and certification, do you also feel this way in other parts of your life?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. It kind of spills over… like with my family, I feel like I’ve let them down too. But mostly it’s tied to feeling like I’m a fake in the apprenticeship and that I’m ruining things people should be able to just handle.\n\nDoctor: If the extra certification were no longer part of your apprenticeship, do you think these feelings would mostly go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it would totally go away. Maybe it’d be less… but I still keep feeling like I’ve already ruined things for everyone, like I’m just faking my way through the apprenticeship. And like I let my family and the younger apprentices down over stuff other people would probably just fix and move on from.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different parts of your life, not just the apprenticeship?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I wouldn’t say it’s everything. Mostly it still comes back to the apprenticeship stuff. Other things bother me sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying about a lot of different parts of my life.\n\nDoctor: For the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not most of the day every day, I don’t think. I’ve had some low moments, but I’m not sure I’d call it depressed like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you tended to avoid social or work situations because you’re afraid people will judge or criticize you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I get uncomfortable sometimes, but I don’t really avoid work or social stuff because of that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I’ll walk past my sketchbook or skip volleyball and just not really care. Even the volunteer group texts, I’ve been ignoring them. Those used to matter more to me.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling sad or down most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve felt off some days, but I don’t know if I’d say sad or down most days.\n\nDoctor: For the past two weeks, have you had little interest or pleasure in things nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much. Most days I just don’t really feel like doing anything with my sketchbook, or volleyball, or even answering the volunteer group. It’s just kind of… not there.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt sad, empty, or hopeless nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe here and there, but I’m not sure I’d say nearly every day. It’s more like I feel kind of blank sometimes.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you had trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I guess I haven’t really noticed it being a big problem… maybe a little, but I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you been feeling guilty or worthless nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I keep feeling like I messed things up for everyone. Like I’m faking my way through the apprenticeship, and I’ve let my family down… and the younger apprentices too. It’s stupid stuff other people would probably just fix and move on from, but I can’t really shake it.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you been feeling tired or low on energy nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I get tired after work sometimes, but I haven’t been keeping track or anything… not sure it’s nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you had trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep’s been off. I used to get by on like three or four hours and still be kind of wired for work and class, but lately I either can’t fall asleep or I wake up before my alarm feeling like I didn’t really sleep.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you been moving or speaking noticeably slower than usual, or feeling unusually restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe slower, I guess. At work I’ve noticed it takes more effort to do basic stuff, like climbing down to grab a fitting or answering the foreman. A couple people asked if I was sick or hungover, so… I guess it’s noticeable.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, has your appetite changed much?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed much. Maybe some days I don’t feel like eating a lot, but… I don’t think it’s been a big change.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
